## Consequential corrections

- 45/72, 1), handwritten quadratic form: H's a xi^2 replaced by the written at_a^2, including lower a.
- 45/73, Stiefel-Whitney display, target exponent: Target of W^i remains the written H^1, not H^i; no mathematical correction inserted.
- 45/75, a), caractéristique 2, on est: Restores live on est, which H treats as cancelled along with 2.
- 45/75, product law, first component of first pair: First factor retains g|f uncertainty; the displayed fg and deg(f) on the right are not used to force f on the left.
- 45/80, (27), determinant, first x index: Retains the written x_1 in determinant instead of supplying the expected running index i.
- 46/35, base-change formula, first letter: Retains the surprising capital H at the start of the base-change expression; does not replace it with the expected lower-case h.
- 53/3, b), interlinear action formula: The inserted action formula is attached to condition b), not to the orbit map in a).
- 53/3, first live Prop, whole proposition: Restores to H the complete criterion relating separatedness of the group scheme to closedness of its unit section.
- 53/3, next Prop., map G x G -> G: Corrects the label from p to pi on the second G x G -> G map.
- 53/3, b), after stable par: Reads G_0 as the written symbol in b), not H’s proposed word générisation.
- 53/3, c), insertion les phi... générisants: Places the inserted generizing-orbit-map condition with c), the specialization assertion.
- 53/3, next Cor., interlinear hypotheses: The quasi-separatedness and pseudotorsor insertion modifies the corollary, not c prime.
- 53/4, upper map G^n to G^m: The first horizontal sign is retained as a dash, with no added arrowhead.
- 53/4, b inverse image formula: The written inverse image is of W, not H’s V; the mathematical surprise remains.
- 53/4, lemma assertion and equivalences: Two written equivalences are restored where H presents questions.
- 53/4, live final proof two maps: The two successive maps G→X and G^0→W are both retained.
- 53/5, height-one paragraph classification: The manuscript says the groups are classified, not merely described.
- 53/5, last quotient: The quotient parameter is ell, not H’s n.
- 53/6, opening inverse image containment: The ambient product is G×W; H adds an unwritten exponent 0.
- 53/6, proposition deletion extent: The initial proposition is not globally cancelled; the later discarded corollary is.
- 53/6, proposition pointwise hypothesis: The written hypothesis is at one point, not on an open subset.
- 53/6, live corollary b group index: The written index is a subscript 0 and the overwritten G is retained.
- 53/6, finite-type proposition inserted H hypothesis: H locally of finite type belongs to the monomorphism proposition.
- 53/6, connected-groups right final qualification: The bounded-dimension qualification on the abelian part is retained.
- 53/7, first corollary hypothesis replacement: The alternative is k′ or A of finite type, not a tensor-product hypothesis.
- 53/7, long left margin conclusion: The marginal conclusion concerns X×_kY; its hypotheses and terminology are retained in full.
- 53/7, condition c base subscript: The base subscript is left doubtful; the ink does not support H’s certain barred k.
- 53/7, proof added conclusion: The first local flatness argument has its written conclusion B regular.
- 53/7, Jacobian criterion inserted integer: The insertion specifies an integer n≥0; it does not insert another neighbourhood U.
- 53/8, implication chain a prime to a: The written a′⇒a implication remains live.
- 53/8, proof initial live maximal point: The maximal-point hypothesis remains live; H cancels it with the discarded wording below.
- 53/8, proof first resulting assertion: The separability of k(g) and normality at g are retained as live assertions.
- 53/9, corollary 2 conclusion: The identification of irreducible and connected components remains a live conclusion.
- 53/9, corollary 3 cancellation: Corollary 3 is cancelled as a whole, including its open-and-closed conclusion.
- 53/9, alternative proof open product: The cancelled alternative proof writes U×U, although the general statement concerns U and V.
- 53/10, proof inserted irreducibility reason: The proof explicitly inserts that f′(Z′) is irreducible.
- 53/10, proof transforms wording and deletion state: The assertion about the π-transforms is live, not cancelled.
- 53/10, counterexample first exponent: The exponent of x remains doubtful, with the visible 7-like form placed first; it is not normalized to fit the counterexample.
- 53/10, table right-torsor insertion: The scheme-side torsor is explicitly qualified as a right torsor.
- 53/10, table torsor choice clause: The identification by a continuous homomorphism is written after choosing e∈E.
- 53/11, opening algebra qualifier: The opening description explicitly retains entière before séparable.
- 53/11, construction type of algebra: The introductory algebra qualifier remains doubtful, now with indétale first from the visible middle ascender.
- 53/11, construction a inductive adjective: The description of A as a limite inductive is live.
- 53/11, construction b later inserted clause: The inserted conclusion is that A bar is ind-étale, not merely réduite.
- 53/12, first b unfinished predicate: The first tensor-product criterion is left syntactically unfinished after est.
- 53/12, algebra introductory field extension: The perfect extension k_1 is introduced for the algebra criteria, not as part of the preceding field criterion c).
- 53/12, Ainsi first réduit deletion state: The live sentence says that every geometrically reduced scheme over k is reduced.
- 53/12, second c target field: The multiplication arrow has a written target k prime, not a missing target.
- 53/12, last introduction extension: The final field extension is separably closed, as written, rather than algebraically closed.
- 53/13, proof a first nonempty hypothesis: The proof explicitly adds non vide to the original X as well as to its scalar extension.
- 53/14, p.ex. inserted hypothesis: The example explicitly carries an inserted non vide hypothesis.
- 53/14, conclusion deleted geometric qualifier: The geometric qualifier in the conclusion is cancelled; H prints it live.
- 53/14, bottom product second factor: The second factor in the final inverse-product formula is now explicitly U/V doubtful, with U favored by its right return.
- 53/15, characterized object: The manuscript says caractériser X; H’s added T is absent from the ink.

- 53/16, Dualité inserted example: H changes the inserted example to G_a; the ink has pour G=G_m. The surprising example is preserved.
- 53/22, translation hypothesis: H replaces the parenthetical disons and leaves the monomorphism condition unread; the written condition is retained.
- 53/22, group-valued functor insertion: The interlinear foncteur en is part of the hypothesis on G prime and is absent from H text.
- 53/22, representability phrase and layout: H omits représentable par and moves the module qualification from line a) into this sentence.

- 53/29, epi: The parenthesis reads épi, not H’s inj.

## Cote 53 — scan 10

Personally read the whole scan and both halves, then every line and both diagrams against C and H. All main blocks were enlarged at 3×, the top diagram at 4×, and individual doubtful or differing groups at 5× or 6×; the black obliteration in condition b was examined at 8×. The cancelled left marginal map was viewed at 6× and rotated clockwise 6° to align the surviving baseline. Formula letters, exponents, primes, arrows, finite and directed limits, the table bracket and inserted torsor qualification were checked. The counterexample exponent remains explicitly doubtful despite the familiar mathematical form. No context was opened.

## Cote 53 — scan 31

The primary reader inspected the whole image and both supplied halves, then seven broad crops at 4× and forty-nine focused crops at 7–14×. A truncated response containing antipodisme, st.bl under sigma, l’algèbre and the next stable was redisplayed; only the redisplayed images count as seen. Twelve supplementary crops covered the complete matrix annotation, the interlinear filtered-limit and group words, the crossed target and formula, direction revisions, tf., the small arc above End, monoïdes and the G forms. Six supplementary crops were rectified by 1–2 degrees (directions and the group insertion counterclockwise 1 degree, crossed target counterclockwise 2 degrees, crossed formula clockwise 1 degree, filtered-limit and tf. clockwise 2 degrees). Every agreed word and mathematical sign was read against the ink; all C doubts and H differences were enlarged. The page inventory was checked top to bottom, including the insertion guides and detached arc. No OCR, context or delegated manuscript reading was used.

## Cote 53 — scan 32

The primary reader inspected the whole scan and both supplied halves before the witnesses, then seven broad crops at 4–8×, two upright margin crops, twenty-seven focused crops at 7–14× and nine supplementary crops at 10–12×. An earlier truncated response did not display the first requested focused images; all twenty-seven were subsequently displayed in small batches and read. The two margins and three supplementary margin details were rotated clockwise 90 degrees, assoc. clockwise 1 degree and the provient insertion clockwise 3 degrees. Every agreed word and mathematical sign was compared with the ink; all C doubts, H differences, cancellations, insertions and diagram endpoints were enlarged. The final inventory was checked top to bottom, including insertion guides, the looped bracket, the extra curve along a margin arrow and its detached mark. No OCR, context or delegated manuscript reading was used.

## Cote 53 — scan 33

The primary reader inspected the whole page and both supplied halves before reading C and H. Eight broad crops at 4–5× and forty-two focused or supplementary crops at 8–16× were then read. The first requested projective crop was truncated in tool output; it was displayed again and actually read before decisions were made. The vertical relation was rotated clockwise 90 degrees, the cancelled lower-left word clockwise 1 degree, and the L insertion twice clockwise 4 degrees. Every agreed word and sign, all C doubts, all H differences, the crossed passages, and the mathematical indices and endpoints were checked against the ink. A second view of the whole page was used to check placement and omissions before the inventory was written. The sequence opening, initially provisionally read as C, was changed only after the focused 9× and 16× views showed the rounded first object and a separate capped shaft. No OCR, context or delegated manuscript reading was used.

## Cote 57 — scan 3

The primary reader inspected the whole sheet, then both supplied halves, then C and H. H has the title immediately before its page marker; that title was included in the comparison. Six broad crops at 3–6× were read line by line, followed by eighteen focused crops at 8–14× of every cancelled or doubtful passage, disputed subscript, mathematical connector, relation, product sign and exponent. The prose is horizontal typescript; the handwritten additions are upright, so no rotation was required on this sheet. Every agreed word, punctuation mark and formula was read against the ink, including the visibly congested letters in k-valued. The inventory distinguishes typing and handwritten additions. No OCR, context, delegated reading or compilation was used.
